2. These bail applications concern 168 crimes
registered in various police stations alleging offences
punishable under Sections 406, 409 and 420 of the Indian
Penal Code and Section 3 r/w 21 (2)(3), 5 r/w 23 of Buds
Act, 2019. In all these bail applications, the complaints
alleged appear to be similar in that deposits have been
made in the four companies named Fashion Gold
International Pvt.Ltd, Nujoom Gold Pvt.Ltd, Qamar Fashion
Gold Pvt.Ltd and Fashion Ornaments Pvt. Ltd.
3. The allegation appears to be that the company and
its Directors induced the complainants to invest money for
business purposes in the companies offering dividends and
they cheated the complainants by neither giving the

B.A.No.5273 & Conn.Cases
553
promised dividends nor repaying the deposits.
4. The learned counsel appearing for the petitioners
and the learned Public Prosecutor were heard.
5. Excepting a few cases, there were interim orders
directing the petitioners to appear before the Investigating
Officer and subject themselves to interrogation, with a
further direction to release them on bail in the event of
their arrest , excepting the cases noted below as the
second group.
6. The learned Public Prosecutor made available a
letter from the Deputy Superintendent of Police, Crime
Branch, Kasargod, stating that in these cases, pursuant to
the orders passed by this Court, in all the cases, the
petitioners had appeared and after interrogation, were
released on bail. There is no complaint of the accused not
cooperating with the investigation. In those cases, the
interim orders granted are made absolute, subject to the
following conditions:
(i) The petitioners shall appear as and when
directed by the Investigating Officer,

B.A.No.5273 & Conn.Cases
554
cooperate with the Investigation and make
available all the documents/information
sought by the Investigating Officer.
(ii) The petitioners shall not intimidate or
attempt to influence the witnesses, tamper
with any evidence, or get involved in similar
offences while on bail.
(iii) The petitioners , directors, accused Nos.5
to 9, – Sri. M.C. Kamarudeen, Sri.T, K Pookkoya
Thangal, Sri. Haris Abdul Khadar, Sri. Sainul
Abideen, Sri. Hisham Ancharappattil-, shall
surrender their passports. If they have no
passports, they shall file affidavits to that effect
before the court concerned on the date of
execution of the bond or within two weeks from
today.
(iv) The petitioners shall furnish their
residential address and their mobile phone
numbers to the Court as well as to the

B.A.No.5273 & Conn.Cases
555
Investigating Officer;
(v) The Directors , accused nos 5 to 9,(names
mentioned above) shall not leave India
without the previous permission of the
jurisdictional court.
(vi) In case of violation of any of the above
conditions, the jurisdictional Court shall be
empowered to consider the application for
cancellation, if any, and pass appropriate
orders in accordance with law,
notwithstanding the bail having been granted
by this Court.
It is made clear that it is within the power of police to
investigate the matter and if necessary to effect recoveries
on the information if any given by the petitioner, even
when the petitioners are on bail as per the judgment of the
Apex Court in Sushila Aggarwal and others V. State
( NCT of Delhi) and another [2021 (1) KHC 663].
8. As far as the second group of cases are concerned,

B.A.No.5273 & Conn.Cases
556
where no interim orders were passed directing surrender
before the Investigating Officer, viz B.A.Nos. 6519/2023,
6520/2023, 8032/2023, 8218/2023, 9374/2023,
10054/2023, 10057/2023, 10059/2023, 10063/2023,
1066/2023, 10067/2023, 10074/2023, 1075/2023,
1077/2023, 10079/2023, 10105/2023, 10108/2023,
10111/2023, 10115/2023, 10117/2023, 10119/2023,
10126/2023, 10129/2023, 10130/2023, 10131/2023,
10133/2023, 10165/2023, 10168/2023, 10169/2023,
10170/2023, 10181/2023, 10183/2023, 10184/2023,
10185/2023, 10186/2023, 10187/2023, 10188/2023,
10193/2023, 10375/2023, 10777/2023, 10779/2023, the
petitioners have not surrendered before the investigating
officer. In view of the orders passed in the connected cases
involving similar crimes, I am inclined to hold that the
custodial interrogation of the petitioners is not necessary.
Accordingly, anticipatory bail is granted, ensuring the
cooperation of the petitioners.
(i) The petitioners, in these bail applications

B.A.No.5273 & Conn.Cases
557
are directed to surrender before the
Investigating Officer within three weeks from
today for interrogation. After interrogation,
they shall be released on bail on executing
separate bonds for Rs.50,000 (Rupees Fifty
Thousand only) each with two solvent
sureties each for the like sum to the
satisfaction of the Investigating Officer on the
following conditions:
(ii) The petitioners shall report before the
Investigating Officer as and when required by
the Investigating Officer.
(iii) The petitioners shall appear as and when
directed by the Investigating Officer,
cooperate with the Investigation and shall
make available all the documents/information
sought for by the Investigating Officer.
(iv) The petitioners shall not intimidate or
attempt to influence the witnesses, tamper
with any evidence, or get involved in similar

B.A.No.5273 & Conn.Cases
558
offences while on bail.
(v) The Directors mentioned above(accused
Nos.5 to 9, – Sri. M.C. Kamarudeen, Sri.T, K
Pookkoya Thangal, Sri. Haris Abdul Khadar, Sri.
Sainul Abideen, Sri. Hisham Ancharappattil-)
shall surrender their passports, if any. If they
have no passports, they shall file affidavits to
that effect before the court concerned on the
date of execution of the bond or within two
weeks from today.
(vi) The petitioners shall furnish their address
and their phone numbers to the Court as well as
to the Investigating Officer;
(vii) The petitioners shall not leave India
without previous permission of the
jurisdictional court.
(viii) In case of violation of any of the above
conditions, the jurisdictional Court shall be
empowered to consider the application for

B.A.No.5273 & Conn.Cases
559
cancellation, if any, and pass appropriate
orders in accordance with the law,
notwithstanding the bail having been
granted by this Court.
It is made clear that it is within the power of police to
investigate the matter and if necessary to effect
recoveries on the information if any given by the
petitioners, even when the petitioners are on bail as per
the judgment of the Apex Court in Sushila Aggarwal and
others V. State ( NCT of Delhi) and another [2021
(1) KHC 663].
The bail applications are allowed as above.

 Sd/-
MOHAMMED NIAS C.P.,
